Have you heard? MatPat’s return, Tubi CEO’s next move, and Trisha Paytas’ payday.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Each week, we handpick a selection of stories to give you a snapshot of trends, updates, business moves, and more from around the creator industry.

This week, a beloved couple has a new channel on YouTube, a vaunted exec has a new gig, and a much-memed creator is making bank (and hitting bookshelves).

Welcome back to YouTube, Matthew and Stephanie Patrick. The man known as MatPat and his wife Stephanie haven’t shared many videos since they sold their Theorist brand nearly four years ago. But these crazy kids couldn’t stay offline forever, so they’re back with PatChat, a new channel featuring the couple’s conversations about internet culture. PatChat won’t upload nearly as frequently as the Theorist channels, and that’s ok. Let the Patricks live a little bit!

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

CNN panelist pushes back against Hasan Piker lawsuit. Piker argued that Scott Jennings defamed him by misconstruing statements that were clearly said in jest. In response, Jennings’ lawyers argued that Piker did make those statements, sarcastic or not — so they are looking to have the $72.5 million lawsuit thrown out. If commentators can be held liable for sarcasm, the internet might be in real trouble.

WhatCultureGaming launches new YouTube channel to pursue different types of content. After building a popular gaming hub, the WhatCulture team is trying something a little different with MegaWhat. The plan is to release original, personality-driven content that will give the WC team more creative freedom while still allowing them to feed the YouTube algorithm on their main channel.

Anjali Sud leaves Tubi to take point at SiriusXM. As CEO of Tubi, Sud led the transformation of the Fox-owned service, which is now a major creator hub. (She was also CEO of Vimeo before that.) Now, Sud is becoming CEO of SiriusXM, giving her a chance to work her magic in the realm of digital radio. Mike Bidgoli will take over for Sud as the new top exec at Tubi HQ.

Elevate Communications brings in TikTok vet to expand creator operations. Elevate is elevating Sam Valentine, who previously connected TikTok with luxury fashion brands as the app’s Head of Digital, Social, and Creator Services. He’ll spearhead digital and social partnerships at Elevate, where he will work with clients that are eager to build creator programs in sectors like sports, entertainment, and healthcare.

Meta brings in well-traveled exec to be the VP of its in-house content studio. After a long stint at NBC News, as well as stops at Goldman Sachs and BlackRock, Rich Latour has arrived at Meta. He will be put in charge of a studio that emphasizes Meta’s brand and highlights its impact across various communities and industries.

Trisha Paytas has a book out (and she makes $100,000 a week on YouTube). While promoting her memoir Crying on the Kitchen Floor, which was recently published by Hachette, Paytas told Andy Cohen that her YouTube earnings can hit six figures during a good week. Oh, and she also has a new dating show that is rolling out on Tubi. Slow down, Trish!

Hulu taps Tinx to revive The Circle (now with TikTok voting). The creator born Christina Najjar is taking the hosting role in a new version of the reality competition series that previously aired on Netflix. Tinx will have big shoes to fill replacing previous host Michelle Buteau, but with help from the voting audience, she can ensure that the Circle revival doesn’t miss a beat.

The first Clio Creator Awards will fete Dhar Mann. Actor Terry Crews will host as the notable advertising award ceremony expands to the realm of creator content. Dhar Mann, YouTube moralist and engineer of many a brand partnership, will receive an inaugural honorary award at the new ceremony.

Kind works with a Savannah Banana to sell a TikTok-exclusive foodstuff. Baseball player Jackson Olson has amassed a significant TikTok following after getting a roster spot on the best-known “Banana Ball” team. Olson is now parlaying his influence to sell a new type of Kind bar that celebrates World Kindness Day — and is only available for purchase via TikTok Shop.

GTA VI codes are flying around on Kick and Twitch ahead of launch day. All of the major platforms want creators to play GTA VI on their services once the game launches, and giving out free copies is a good way to engender that loyalty. At this point, I’m wondering if GTA VI will be able to live up to its immersurable hype, or if viewers will tire of it after it because the main topic of conversation everywhere.

Barbie and Hot Wheels are on TikTok Shop this holiday season. TikTok continues to onboard name brands as it prepares for its most lucrative holiday season shopping spree. So if someone on your list has their eyes on some Mattel products, deals on those items will be hitting TikTok Shop in no time.

Celebs are consenting to having their voices cloned for AI ads. Ogilvy and CAA have teamed up for a partnership that will make a-list talent more accessible for advertisers and agencies that rely on generative AI pipelines. George Clooney is a CAA client, but if he goes along with this, I’m going to have no choice but to start calling him George Cloney.

Shaq is good at dunking, but can he hide in plain sight? The Big Diesel has a new gig: An ABC series that will ask contestants to hide the 7’1″ goliath in plain sight. I think, to be fair, the contestants should get to start by hiding Kevin Hart before working their way up to Shaq.

Another impulse spending deterrent is taking off in China. The WeChat mini-app dubbed ‘Wallet, Please Wait’ lets users decide whether strangers will be able to hold off on big purchases. I would be more interested in app that lets me vote on whether a stranger should be able to tell a stranger how to spend their money.

Martin McDonagh Calls Sam Rockwell “One of the Best Actors of His Generation, If Not the Best”

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Director Martin McDonagh has become as well-known for making memorable films as he is for writing the complex male friendships within them.

"I like mining down into the sensitivity that I think a lot of male relationships do have, but aren't as – especially in American films – explored sensitively," he said about the subject during a panel conversation at the inaugural Vanity Fair Screening Room at LACMA on Friday. "It's not just a buddy film, and it's not a romance, and it's not just about cool CIA guys," McDonagh continued. “It's sort of the opposite in other ways. And that I think is interesting to explore because I think my male relationships are that way. They can be sensitive and sad and happy and all the things that a female relationship can be. So I think that's why I keep coming back to it.”

Martin McDonagh at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

The conversation, which featured McDonagh and the film’s stars John Malkovich and Mariana di Girolamo, was moderated by VF's Senior Hollywood Correspondent Rebecca Ford following an exclusive screening of their new film, Wild Horse Nine. The acclaimed dark comedy follows two CIA agents (Malkovich and Sam Rockwell) who are not only behind the plan to overthrow the Chilean government in 1973, but have also had a hand in several other significant historical moments in the US and abroad.

Maria di Girolamo, John Malkovich and Martin McDonagh, in conversation with Rebecca Ford at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on Friday, November 13, 2026.

Malkovich and Rockwell's characters engage in pithy, often hilarious banter throughout the film, and their onscreen chemistry is a highlight.

John Malkovich at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

"I love Sam," said Malkovich. “I knew him a bit socially before this, but I'd worked with him once before, which was a great pleasure on a film called A Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy. He's like a kind of expert trainer at SeaWorld who can get the whales and the sharks and the dolphins to show things in exchange for a fish. And Sam is a great student of acting. He is a great student of film and of theater, and he's great fun, very hardworking, and just an ideal colleague. I couldn't imagine a better colleague than Sam and I've had some excellent colleagues.”

"He's one of the best actors of his generation, if not the best," adds McDonagh of Rockwell. "He's always surprising, always dangerous, always sensitive, always smart, always coming up with new stuff. And I think there's a danger to him that's lovely to write for. We always hope the characters will always be surprising, and with Sam's voice in the head, that really helps."

Mariana di Girolamo at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

For rising star Mariana di Girolamo, a Chilean actress making her English language movie debut with Wild Horse Nine, she was excited and only slightly intimidated to be a part of the cast.

"It was quite a challenge to perform for the first time in English," she said. "I prepared myself with a coach and we rehearsed with John, Sam and Parker [Posey] and Ailin [Salas] in a public theater in New York where I heard the script for the first time. That was amazing for me, for us." di Girolamo, who is very familiar with the fraught period of Chilean history depicted in the film, said she appreciated how McDonagh put it all in context. "It was my first time approaching that period of time in this way with this very beautiful dark humor. I was laughing about these things and feeling empathy with these CIA agents that committed a lot of horrific, horrific things. I've never read something like that, and the arc of my character is so beautiful, unique, I really wanted to be part of this."

Maria di Girolamo, John Malkovich and Martin McDonagh, in conversation with Rebecca Ford at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on Friday, November 13, 2026.

The inaugural edition of Vanity Fair's Screening Room pairs this year’s most compelling films with a slate of programming that includes post-screening panels and conversations with actors and filmmakers. The invitation-only events are welcoming an audience from the worlds of film, fashion, culture, politics, media, and tech, much like the magazine itself. VF editor Daniel Kile introduced the first screening, welcoming the audience to the Steve Tisch Theater at LACMA, which is not yet open to the public.

Following the program in Los Angeles, Vanity Fair’s Screening Room will arrive in New York, where screenings and events will take place at Cherry Lane Theatre from December 4–6.

Fallout 5 and Fallout Remasters Officially Announced

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xbox wants more Fallout, and after a yearslong drought of new games, Bethesda Softworks is making it happen. Bethesda plans to release remasters of both Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas for modern platforms, the company said in July in a massive roadmap of the developer's future. Those remasters are part of a larger plan to reinvest in Fallout games — including Fallout 5, which is currently in pre-production — and a new untitled project at Obsidian Entertainment.

"We have multiple Fallout projects in active development right now," Bethesda said in an official announcement this summer. That includes Fallout 5, which is described as the studio's "long-range destination," and "a major Fallout 76 expansion" planned for 2027 called Raven Rock, which will serve as a prequel story to Fallout 3.

Bethesda also confirmed two long-standing rumors, announcing remasters for Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas. The studio did not provide any further details or a release window, but the projects have reportedly been in the works for many years. The existence of a Fallout 3 remaster dates back to at least 2020, when the project's existence was revealed through court documents related to the Federal Trade Commission's (FTC) litigation regarding Microsoft's buyout of Activision Blizzard.

Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas' remasters follow last year's release of The Elder Scrolls 4: Oblivion Remastered, which was co-developed by Virtuos and Bethesda Game Studios. Bethesda also marked the 10-year anniversary of Fallout 4 in 2025, with the release of Fallout 4: Anniversary Edition. That rerelease included the original game, all six of the previously released DLC add-ons, as well as over 150 items pulled from the Fallout 4 Creation Club.

Fallout 3 was originally released in 2008 for PlayStation 3, Windows PC, and Xbox 360. The game was the third major installment in the Fallout series, and the first to be developed by Bethesda after acquiring the rights to the franchise from Interplay Entertainment. Fallout 3 is set in and around Washington D.C. 200 years after a devastating nuclear war, and players control a 19-year-old Vault dweller in search of their missing father.

Fallout: New Vegas followed in 2010, and was developed by Obsidian Entertainment. It was set in the Mojave Desert and casts players as a courier who becomes embroiled in a conflict between various factions who are vying for control of the region.

In addition to new Fallout games, a third season of the Fallout TV series is in the works and recently wrapped filming. Bethesda Game Studios is also hard at work on the next game in its fantasy role-playing game series: The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel is now in development.

Former Obsidian dev Chris Avellone dishes on contentious conversations with Bethesda after Dead Money

SpaceX’s Starship Just Did Something for Only the 2nd Time. Saturday Could Be Even Bigger.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Starship reached orbit with 26 Starlink satellites on Flight 15, but the booster was diverted into the water and burst into flames.

Space Exploration Technologies Corp.'s (SPCX -0.59%) Starship Flight 15 lifted off from Starbase, Texas, on Friday, Nov. 13, at 5:47 p.m. ET in the company's attempt to pull off a "double-catch" of both stages of its Starship rocket for the first time.

But after a clean separation in the upper atmosphere, the lower half -- a Super Heavy booster -- was diverted off the coast, splashing down in the Gulf of Mexico, after which it burst into flames. The company has not shared details of the issue encountered.

While that kept the mission from achieving complete success, Starship — the upper stage — successfully reached orbit at 6:13 p.m. ET. That's only the second time the company's pulled that off, with SpaceX's Dan Huot calling it "a really great moment" on Flight 15's livestream.

The payload of 26 of Starlink's latest satellites is currently being deployed.

If the rest of the mission goes according to plan, Starship will reenter the atmosphere tomorrow with a catch attempt scheduled for 4:17 p.m. ET. SpaceX said before launch that it would attempt a catch only if all systems needed to guide it through the atmosphere and into the tower successfully were healthy. So, successfully reaching orbit doesn't guarantee that the catch will go ahead.

Even without a first catch, a successful second catch of Starship would be huge for the company, representing a major step toward making SpaceX's goal of a fully and rapidly reusable, heavy lift rocket a reality. The program is a major part of SpaceX's growth thesis.

In the third quarter, SpaceX brought in $14.6 billion in revenue, and the Space segment accounted for just $1.8 billion of that. Starship isn't really about the company's top line -- for the moment, that is.

In the near term, an operational Starship program's main job would be to get as many Starlink satellites into orbit as quickly and cheaply as possible. Long term, however, once the program scales and the bulk of the Starlink satellite network has been filled out, Starship would allow the company to greatly expand its launch service business, and the top-line will undoubtedly grow significantly.

The company's current workhorse, Falcon 9, reuses its booster but throws away its upper stage on every flight. The ability to reuse such a large rocket would drive down the cost of getting payloads of all sizes into space, helping it win larger contracts from enterprise and government customers alike.

SpaceX is aiming for full Starship capability around the end of 2027, but the program has seen significant setbacks and delays in its development. A successful catch tomorrow would go a long way in reassuring investors worried about further delays.

Getting Starship into orbit with a full load of Starlink satellites is a good day for SpaceX, but having the booster diverted into the water and burst into flames is a reminder that the hard part of this program -- the real essence of what makes it special -- is not getting to space, but getting back to Earth in one piece, over and over. That is what will change the economics of space.

Lakers' Austin Reaves day-to-day with bruised heel, doesn't undergo imaging

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Any Los Angeles Lakers game in which Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves both exit with injuries is going to be concerning, but it appears the team has dodged any serious fallout.

Two days after the Lakers' two leading scorers left a win over the Milwaukee Bucks, head coach JJ Redick told reporters Friday that he expects Dončić to be available for Sunday's game against the Phoenix Suns. Dončić had already told reporters he was "good" the night of the injury, which was a tweaked ankle.

As for Reaves, Redick said the guard was day-to-day with a bruise on his heel, but the matter is apparently minor enough the team didn't even bother with imaging on the foot.

"AR, based on his physical evaluation and how he was feeling yesterday, we opted against imaging. He'll be day-to-day. We'll have him on the report tomorrow at 5 o'clock."

Even with their early exits Friday, no pair of teammates is averaging more com,bined points per game than Dončić and Reaves. Dončić leads the league in scoring with 38.6 points per game, while Reaves if 15th with 24.7. Dončić is also ninth in the league in assists per game at 7.6.

Rotation guard Collin Sexton also exited Friday's game a collision with Bucks guard Ryan Rollins. He needed stitches above his right eye, but Redick said a concussion was ruled out and he's "probably in the same boat" as Reaves.

The Lakers-Suns game is scheduled for 1 p.m. PT on Sunday in Phoenix.

Trump Says Federal Film Tax Credit Is Gaining Momentum

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The proposal for film and TV tax incentives, which drew broad bipartisan support when it was introduced earlier this year, faces an uphill battle for passage in the lame-duck session.

After a sweeping loss at the midterms for Republicans dimmed the short-term prospects of a federal film and TV tax credit, President Trump is pushing for passage of legislation for production subsidies.

Trump on Friday said he’s working with lawmakers on both sides of the aisle on the legislation.

“The support and popularity of doing this, and bringing Entertainment back to the United States of America, has been unbelievable!” Trump wrote on Truth Social.

A bipartisan group of lawmakers has introduced proposed legislation to establish a federal film and TV tax credit, aimed at reshoring production to the U.S. amid a tit-for-tat battle among countries to host Hollywood.

If passed, the Motion Picture, Television, and Entertainment Revitalization Act would establish a 20 percent base tax credit on labor expenses, rising to a maximum of 30 percent with bonuses. Only projects with a budget over $1 million, where 75 percent of filming occurs in the U.S., would be eligible for the credit. The subsidy would be stackable on top of individual tax incentives from states, much like the system in several countries that’ve lured production away from the U.S. in recent years.

In Friday’s post, Trump said that the entertainment industry has “largely moved to Canada, the United Kingdom, Australia and other countries.”

“The actors, producers, and all others involved want to come back home!” Trump added. “Importantly, hundreds of thousands of Blue Collar Workers will be positively affected, BIG TIME, by the passage of this Bill.”

The post follows Democrats preparing investigations into the Trump administration, raising questions about whether they will focus on film subsidies during the lame-duck session. The tax credit would cost billions of dollars, money that Democrats may be hesitant to attach to a short-term bill during the year-end budget fight.

Still, Trump stressed that Democrats are open to the legislation since “much of the money being lost to other countries is coming out of the Blue States.”

“The amount of money spent on these Tax Incentives will be made up tenfold by the money pouring into the Treasury’s coffers,” he added. “Meetings are being set up, routinely, with the Leaders of both Parties in order to, GET THIS DONE!”

Trump doesn’t plan to raise Nvidia, spy issue during China trip: U.S. officials

📅 2026-11-14  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

President t Donald Trump is not expected to raise allegations of a Chinese intelligence operation involving former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during his upcoming meeting with Chinese President Xi Jinping, senior U.S. officials said Friday.

Officials also said that Elon Musk, the CEO of Tesla and SpaceX, will accompany Trump on the trip to Shenzhen, China, for the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit on Nov. 18 and 19.

The White House, earlier Friday, said Trump plans to have a three-way summit there with Xi and Russian President Vladimir Putin.

Jensen Huang's relationship with the suspected spy, Alice Huang, who is not related to him, has raised concerns that a Singapore-based company where she was an executive diverted restricted Nvidia chips to China.

"The situation with Nvidia should not really have any bearing on the trip," an official said on the press call, speaking on the condition that they not be identified.

"In all of our discussions and negotiations with the Chinese, we do not negotiate American export controls ... and we don't expect it to figure at all in any of our conversations with the Chinese while we're there."

Asked if Trump would raise the allegations against Alice Huang to Chinese leader Xi Jinping, the official said, "The focus of a meeting with President Xi, at least on the economic side, is going to be about implementation of the trade agreement."

"When it comes to some of these national security matters and examinations and investigations that are going on in the U.S., [that is] probably not something that we're going to read the Chinese in on at this moment," the official said.

Another official said, "I don't expect the president to raise it — the Alice Huang issue."

China has called the allegations against Alice Huang baseless.

Huang resigned this week as Nvidia's CEO after damning reports that he had an affair with Alice Huang, who was an executive of Megaspeed, a cloud-computing company that is being eyed for possible facilitation of sending billions of dollars worth of Nvidia chips in violation of U.S. restrictions on those chips.

The scandal has rocked Nvidia and raised questions about whether the Commerce Department, which is led by Howard Lutnick, has been aggressive enough at blocking advanced chips from ending up in China.

During Friday's call, a reporter asked why Musk was going on the trip with Trump.

"I don't want to speak for the president on exactly why Elon's going," an official said.

"Obviously, he has tremendous business interests in the U.S.-China relationship. He's a big part of our national security," the official said. "There are numerous reasons, given the engagement that the president is going to have, where I think Elon would be of tremendous benefit, just even as an advisor to the president."

SpaceX launches Starship in key test flight of massive rocket

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a key test flight on Friday, SpaceX tried but failed to recover both stages of its massive Starship rocket.

The rocket lifted off from the company's South Texas launch facility at 4:47 p.m. local time, or 5:47 p.m. ET. SpaceX had to abort its plans to catch and reuse the booster, instead diverting it to splash into the Gulf of Mexico, where it burst into flames.

Test Flight 15, the most ambitious Starship mission to date, was the first time Elon Musk's company attempted to catch both the booster and upper stage, or spacecraft portion, of the massive rocket.

Earlier on Friday, SpaceX obtained authorization from the Federal Aviation Administration. Hours later, the FAA issued a nationwide ground stop after reporting communication problems affecting "multiple" air traffic control centers. The SpaceX license wasn't effected by the flight stoppage, a spokesperson for the FAA told CNBC.

The upper stage of the Starship continued its trajectory into orbit after the company failed to catch and salvage the booster. The rocket is expected to fly around the Earth fourteen times in total. If successful, SpaceX will land the upper stage back at its Starbase facility on Saturday.

Starship's success is critical to SpaceX and its ambitions to eventually lift cargo and people to the surface of the Moon and Mars. In the near-term, SpaceX needs to send more satellites into orbit to expands its Starlink satellite internet service, which is currently the company's profit center.

The company's constellation already includes over 11,000 active satellites in low Earth orbit. One Starship rocket has the capacity to carry up to 60 Starlink v3 satellites into orbit, the company has said, representing about 20 times the capacity of earlier Falcon 9 rockets.

In the previous Starship test flight, the company released 26 v3 satellites, and released another 26 in the Friday test flight, with the goal of "Starlink becoming 51% of the internet," SpaceX said in a post on X.

When it comes to launches, "SpaceX is likely a full decade ahead of peers on this front," William Blair analysts wrote in a note out on Friday. "Even if tonight's catch is unsuccessful, a delay by a few months is not that consequential."

Meanwhile, SpaceX is facing fresh criticism over restrictions imposed on its Starshield satellite internet service in Ukraine. Starshield is built on Starlink technology and is the military-tier of SpaceX's satellite internet service.

Earlier this week, Ukraine President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said his country's military forces had experienced limits on Starshield without warning again, harming their ability to defend against Russia's ongoing aerial attacks. Similar limits on Starshield in Ukraine were temporarily imposed last month.

Some U.S. senators have condemned the restrictions, and urged Congress to investigate SpaceX.

FAA grounds departing flights nationwide, citing air traffic communication system outage

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Federal Aviation Administration halted thousands of departing flights nationwide on Friday afternoon, a rare measure, blaming communications issues that affected "multiple" air traffic control centers. More than 10,000 flights were disrupted.

Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin said Friday at the White House that there was no indication that the issues were a threat to the United States.

The FAA said in post on X that there was a "temporary telecommunications outage" and that it halted flights out of an "abundance of caution." The issues were related to communications and radar problems, according to an internal message from Alaska Airlines, which was seen by CNBC.

Airlines said en route flights would continue, but carriers were preparing to cancel flights as the disruption wore on. JetBlue Airways told staff it would cancel the remainder of the day's flights, aside from European routes, as well as others scheduled for Saturday so that crews and planes are in place to resume operations, according to a memo seen by CNBC.

FAA administrator Bryan Bedford had a call with airline CEOs around 6 p.m. indicating that it was unclear when the situation would be resolved, according to people familiar with the matter.

As of 5:56 p.m. ET, some outbound international traffic was in the air, according to Flightradar24, flight-tracking site. That included a Lufthansa flight from Boston to Frankfurt, Germany, that departed at 5:21 p.m. ET and an ITA Airways flight from Boston to Rome that left at 5:24 p.m. ET, according to the site.

As of 6:50 p.m. ET, cargo and passenger flights were also departing the West Coast for Asia and Hawaii.

Flights will be allowed to depart as en route traffic facilities recover, "through coordination with the air traffic control system," the FAA said at about 6 p.m..

Airlines said flights in the air would continue.

When it issued the ground stop at about 4 p.m., the FAA said the communications issues were "impacting multiple Air Route Traffic Control Centers." These facilities guide airplanes at high altitudes along their routes.

The FAA's technicians are investigating, the agency said.

A ground stop halts flights at their departure point. Disruptions tend to snowball if ground stops drag on as flight crews reach their maximum number of duty hours for a day.

There were 11,000 flights scheduled to depart U.S. airports after 4 p.m. ET on Friday, according to flight-data firm Cirium. There were about 600 diverted flights on Friday, 104 of them sent to other airports, Cirium said.

Nationwide ground stops are extremely rare.

In January 2023, the FAA halted flights because of issues with a pilot alert system. Before that, traffic was halted nationwide after the Sept. 11, 2001, terror attacks.

Technical issues have repeatedly snarled air traffic in the U.S. in recent years.

In September, hundreds of flights into Newark, New Jersey, and Philadelphia were canceled, delayed or diverted after a Verizon fiber cable was accidentally cut. Officials said it occurred during construction in New Jersey, severing a backup feed of key data for air traffic controllers.

In 2025, air traffic controllers lost contact with aircraft heading to and from Newark Liberty International Airport, an equipment failure that led to massive flight delays and raised more concerns about aging U.S. aviation infrastructure and staffing shortages.

It wasn't immediately clear what caused Friday's problem.

Inbound international flights will be managed on a case by case basis, the FAA said.

The major U.S. airlines issued statements saying they would work with customers who were affected by the ground stop and encouraged travelers to check for updates.

— CNBC's Kevin Breuninger and Phil LeBeau contributed to this article.

Thailand SEC proposes 5 million Baht daily cap on stablecoin transfers — Why?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) has proposed a set of new rules to regulate stablecoin transactions conducted through licensed digital asset operators.

Announced on 12th November, the draft notification aims to prevent cryptocurrencies and stablecoins from being used for money laundering, cybercrime, and bypassing regulations governing international money transfers.

Here, it’s worth noting that this update came soon after September’s public consultation. During the same, the regulator collected feedback from industry participants.

As it stands, the SEC has incorporated those changes into the latest draft. It is now seeking further public comments until 26th November.

In this, one of the key proposals is to introduce a daily transfer limit of 5 million Thai baht per customer at each regulated digital asset operator. That said, the limit would apply separately to incoming and outgoing stablecoin transfers. However, the value of these transactions must also be consistent with the customer’s declared income and financial position.

However, it’s important to note that transfers between a customer’s own accounts at two SEC-supervised operators would be exempt from the limit, provided both operators follow the Travel Rule.

Simultaneously, the SEC also wants to strengthen wallet ownership verification. In this, the SEC has enabled customers to deposit stablecoins from their own verified wallets and withdraw them to wallets registered in their names.

Additionally, operators would also need to follow the Travel Rule, analyze customers, identify suspicious accounts, and track transactions connected to high-risk wallets.

These requirements would be done in order to make it harder for criminals to move illicit funds through regulated platforms.

Finally, the proposal also introduces stricter responsibilities for exchanges and brokers. Exchanges would have to publicly disclose their market makers and the digital assets for which they provide liquidity. Meanwhile, brokers would be prohibited from using liquidity providers for stablecoin-to-Thai baht trading pairs.

Besides that, brokers and dealers offering off-platform transactions would face a minimum transaction threshold of 3 million baht and would have to publish trading prices for transparency. Brokers would no longer be allowed to arrange off-platform trades directly between customers.

Taken together, these steps would help the SEC combat crimes that are concerning the stablecoin ecosystem in Thailand.

Ishika Kumari is a Crypto Analyst at AMBCrypto, specializing in regulatory developments, market dynamics, and blockchain’s real-world impact. She breaks down complex protocols and legislation into practical, easy-to-understand insights.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/trump-administration-delays-enforcement-medicaid-work-requirements-six-months" hreflang="en">Trump administration delays enforcement of Medicaid work requirements for six months</a>

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →
Trump administration delays enforcement of Medicaid work requirements for six months" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Trump administration is giving states an optional six-month grace period for enforcing new Medicaid work requirements, which were due to go into effect January 1.

The Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) announced Friday an optional "hold harmless" period through June 30, 2027, to give states a transition period to further modernize systems, improve processes and streamline the applicant and beneficiary experience.

During the "hold harmless" period, states will still assess whether Medicaid applicants and beneficiaries meet the work requirements or qualify for an exclusion, but otherwise eligible people will not lose or be denied coverage solely because they fail to meet the requirement, CMS said.

CMS says the transition is intended to give states time to improve eligibility systems, automate verification, train workers and improve the beneficiary experience.

"States have made real progress, and they’ve been clear with us about what it takes to get this right for beneficiaries—at scale and for the long term," Dan Brillman, Deputy Administrator and Director of the Center for Medicaid and CHIP Services, said in a statement. "If the state can use data to show that someone is working or qualifies for an exclusion, that person should not have to track down paperwork to prove it. And when someone needs support, they should know exactly what to do, when to do it, and where to go. That’s how we build a Medicaid program that works better for people, connects them to work, education, and training opportunities, and supports paths to prosperity."

During the six-month enforcement pause, CMS encourages states to use existing data to automatically verify qualifying activities and exemptions before requesting additional information from beneficiaries.

States are also encouraged to simplify notices and make the process easy to navigate so beneficiaries understand what the state has already verified, what information is still needed and how to respond or get help. States should also test eligibility systems end-to-end, particularly how the systems handle missing, outdated or conflicting information, to resolve operational issues and minimize inappropriate coverage disruptions.

CMS also called on states to coordinate with community partners to connect applicants and beneficiaries who need assistance with opportunities for employment, education, job training and volunteering.

The work requirements were established as part of last summer's massive H.R. 1 legislation, or the One Big Beautiful Bill Act. Under the mandate, certain Medicaid applicants and beneficiaries ages 19 to 64 must meet a statutory work requirement, unless excluded. There are exemptions in place for the medically frail, disabled and others who are unable to meet the mandates.

CMS’ June 2026 interim final rule outlines qualifying activities, including 80 hours per month of work, community service or participation in a work program; enrollment in an educational program at least half-time; a combination of qualifying activities totaling at least 80 hours per month; or meeting the specified monthly income threshold.

State Medicaid administrators, health insurers, physicians and hospitals have pushed back on the Medicaid work requirements, citing concerns about financial and operational challenges, a spike in uncompensated care and the rule's strict "medical frailty" exemptions.

Industry groups argue the requirements impose onerous documentation burdens. The Alliance of Community Health Plans said that the final rule adds documentation requirements and tightens criteria for exemptions to the requirements, which will make it harder for individuals who need coverage to secure exemptions they may qualify for.

The organization also noted that most individuals enrolled in Medicaid are already working or in school.

Jennifer Tolbert, deputy director of the program on Medicaid and the uninsured at KFF, said in a quick take post back in June that "the rule adopts a restrictive definition of medical frailty that differs from states’ early expectations."

"Given the complexity of the provisions in the rule, states will likely face significant challenges in operationalizing the requirements in the next six months," Tolbert wrote. "With the short implementation timeline, states were already making implementation decisions that may not align with the rule."

Studies estimate that between 5 million and 10 million people could lose Medicaid coverage in 2028 following the rollout of new work requirements.

The Trump administration's tight definitions of medical frailty and documentation burdens have triggered multiple lawsuits from states, medical organizations and advocacy groups. A coalition of 26 states sued the Trump administration over its rollout of Medicaid work requirements. arguing that key provisions could cause eligible people to lose coverage and disrupt states’ implementation efforts. The states argue CMS’ new definition of who qualifies as “medically frail” is too restrictive and could make it harder for people with serious health conditions to receive exemptions.

In October, a Massachusetts judge sided with the states and rejected the Trump administration's definition of "medical frailty" for critical exemptions.

A group of physician groups, individual Medicaid enrollees and the city of Columbus also sued the federal government to challenge the work requirements.

State Medicaid agencies have warned that the accelerated IT and administrative build required by January 1, 2027, is putting severe pressure on local welfare offices and safety-net hospital operating margins, as Senior Writer Anastassia Gliadkovskaya reported.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/commonspirit-health-logs-544m-operating-loss-begin-its-fy2027" hreflang="en">CommonSpirit Health logs $544M operating loss to begin its FY2027</a>

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
CommonSpirit Health logs $544M operating loss to begin its FY2027" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

CommonSpirit Health kicked off its 2027 fiscal year with a $544 million operating loss as decreasing acuity, dampening payer mix and other expenses outpaced the large nonprofit’s strong volumes and improving operating efficiency, according to financial results for the quarter ended Sept. 30.

That operating loss excludes $65 million non-routine special charges incurred by the health system—most notably severance costs and other fees tied to its strategic, multi-billion-dollar decision to end a long-term revenue cycle contract with Tenet Healthcare—during the quarter, and reflect a -5.3% operating margin. The system had reported a $396 million operating loss and -4% margin during the same period a year prior.

Earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization (EBITDA) was $77 million in the quarter, also a dip from the prior year’s $129 million.

“Our first-quarter performance reflects steady operational progress, even amid persistent cost and reimbursement headwinds, as patients continue to choose CommonSpirit for quality care,” Chief Financial Officer Michael P. Browning said in a release.

CommonSpirit, already one of the country’s largest health systems, said it grew its quarterly operating revenue 5.3% year over year to $10.3 billion. The uptick included a 2.8% year-over-year increase in adjusted admissions as well as $165 million in net income from the California provider fee program, as opposed to no such benefit recorded the year prior. At the same time, the organization said its revenues were affected by “revenue yield challenges” and increases in self-pay, Medicare and Medicaid among its payer mix—both issues cited in recent financial reports from other major health systems.

Meanwhile, CommonSpirit’s operating expenses (before special charges) rose 6.6% year over year to nearly $10.9 billion. Much of the increase was tied to higher volumes as well as salary inflation, the latter of which came even as the organization improved its labor productivity (hospital full-time equivalents per adjusted admission decreased by 1.4%) and cut down its contract labor spend.

CommonSpirit’s results also included $115 million of net nonoperating income, substantially less than the year before due to “market fluctuations” affecting its investment returns. That left a bottom-line net loss of $496 million for the quarter and, as of Sept. 30, 160 days of cash on hand.

Management, in the filing and an accompanying press release, noted that the quarter’s numbers land as the system continues to execute on a sweeping operating performance improvement plan. That effort includes work to boost volumes through a focus on its care network, revenue realization efforts surrounding denials and managed care negotiations, standardizing department staffing models to trim labor costs and potential service line rationalization “for those markets where performance is currently below requirements.”

“We are making deliberate, near-term investments to unify our electronic health record, enterprise resource planning and revenue cycle platforms, while advancing responsible AI adoption to strengthen our system for the long term,” Browning said. “Keeping patients at the center of everything we do requires disciplined expense management and fair, timely reimbursement for the care we deliver.”

“Financial sustainability is what makes our mission possible,” added President and CEO Wright Lassiter III in the release. “Through disciplined operations, we are supporting our team members, strengthening our ministry and protecting access to compassionate, high-quality care across the communities we serve.”

CommonSpirit Health comprises about 2,600 care sites, including 132 hospitals, and more than 160,000 employees across 24 states. In the most recent fiscal year ended June 30, it reported $42.4 billion in total operating revenue and a $430 million operating loss (-1% operating margin) before special charges, an improvement from the prior fiscal year.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/plan-sponsor-expectations-policy-shifts-set-pressure-payers-2027" hreflang="en">Plan sponsor expectations, policy shifts set to pressure payers in 2027</a>

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Plan sponsor expectations, policy shifts set to pressure payers in 2027" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

2027 is shaping up to be a complex year for health plans, as demands from clients to demonstrate value get louder, according to a new report.

Analysts at consulting firm Numerof & Associates released their 2027 outlook report for the insurance industry, and said that in a world where the cost of healthcare continues to rise, more patients and plan sponsors are asking questions about what plans are bringing to the table.

Increasing transparency does make it easier for members to make their own healthcare decisions, but that alone isn't enough to resolve affordability challenges, per the report. It means that insurers will be tasked with engaging differently with consumers and transforming their experiences.

"The bigger issue is not just that healthcare costs keep rising but the traditional insurance model is becoming increasingly tough to sustain without demonstrating better value," said Michael Abrams, co-founder and managing partner at Numerof, in an interview. "Consumers and employers are getting more sensitized to the issue of value."

That interest in finding greater value is pushing employers to consider alternative models and approaches that can manage benefit costs, such as direct provider contracting, onsite clinics and care navigation programs.

The expectation is not just lower costs, but to drive better outcomes and have greater accountability over the medical benefit offerings, per the report. And this trend opens the door for new entrants to thrive, and challenge the role of health plans as the bridge between employers and providers.

Abrams said digital health companies that target certain specialty areas, such as musculoskeletal care or diabetes, have a significant opportunity to fill that gap.

"These digital healthcare companies aren’t necessarily replacing insurers; they’re competing for functions that insurers traditionally performed or should be performing," he said.

In addition, these trends are gaining steam at a time of significant policy upheaval, further complicating the outlook for health plans, according to the report. The expiration of the enhanced premium tax credits for Affordable Care Act plans has driven up costs, and Medicaid work requirements taking effect on Jan. 1 could significantly grow the uninsured or underinsured population.

Reimbursement trends in other markets, such as Medicare Advantage, are also in the mix, the report said.

Abrams said the "convergence" of those policy changes with increased pressure from clients and members makes for a uniquely challenging combination.

"Insurers are getting squeezed from a number of different things all at the same time," he said.

The report recommends that plans looking to survive in this complex time should focus on demonstrating their value to plan sponsors and enrollees, particularly around managing the total cost of care. Insurers should also rethink how they engage with members to support a stronger relationship with consumers.

It also means finding ways to think differently about their relationship with providers; a step that will be critical to addressing the cost of care. Payers should look for provider partners that can take on risk, and work proactively with providers to drive better outcomes and improve value.

Abrams said that payer-provider dynamics are often coercive rather than collaborative. In one market, the insurer may be more powerful and in another the provider, but in either case it's based around a show of dominance rather than working together.

"With continued consolidation on both sides, we have seen a kind of polarization where the only mechanism for resolution is who has the biggest market clout," Abrams said.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/ardent-health-acquiring-missouri-nonprofit-boone-health-47m" hreflang="en">Ardent Health acquiring Missouri nonprofit Boone Health for $47M</a>

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
Ardent Health acquiring Missouri nonprofit Boone Health for $47M" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Ardent Health is acquiring Boone Health, a mid-sized nonprofit system based in Columbia, Missouri, for $47 million in cash, the organizations announced Thursday. It marks Ardent's first entry into the Missouri healthcare market.

The deal, expected to close in the first quarter of 2027, includes a 10-year, $200 million capital commitment on nonprofit Ardent’s part. That money will be invested in Boone’s facilities, workforce and, in the near term, an expansion of its emergency department, according to the announcement.

Ardent has agreed to maintain Boone’s essential hospital services while developing its 392-bed regional referral center, Boone Hospital Center. It also plans to maintain employment for existing Boone employees and providers, continue its charity care policy and establish a local advisory board.

“We are acquiring a strong asset in an attractive and growing midsize market with significant opportunity to drive operating and financial improvement, all while delivering high patient quality care and safety,” Ardent Health President and CEO Dave Caspers said during a Thursday afternoon investor call discussing the deal. “It also represents an effective use of capital at a compelling valuation that we expect will create long-term shareholder value.”

Alongside its main hospital, Ardent would acquire Boone’s medical group, 19 care sites including an outpatient campus and five medical office buildings, related ancillary services and real estate. The system is the second-largest provider in its region with about 30% market share and half a billion dollars of annual revenue, Caspers said, with a local population that is well insured (93% coverage) and growing well ahead of the national average (3.4% in the next five years).

“Boone expands our footprint into a new attractive market,” Caspers explained. “It diversifies our business and enhances scale. Further, adding Boone's regional system to our portfolio is consistent with our strategy of building leading healthcare platforms in growing mid-sized communities. We're highly aligned with Boone Health on culture and mission, including putting the patient at the center of everything we do, with an aim toward best-in-class clinical care, patient quality, safety and access to services.”

The executive noted to investors that Boone’s facilities are relatively modern and well maintained, allowing Ardent to direct more capital toward its growth. The company has also identified “meaningful opportunities to optimize financial performance.” By leveraging its larger scale and expertise to tackle costs (supply chain procurement, vendor consolidation) and revenue cycle processes, Ardent executives said they expect to generate about $5 million to $10 million additional EBITDA within the deal’s first year, and at least $30 million of annual synergies three years after the deal’s close.

Boone Health has been independent since 2021, following over three decades during which it operated as part of the BJC HealthCare system. In 2025, its leadership kicked off a search for potential strategic partners “that could provide additional capital and resources to support the system's continued growth. Ardent was selected after a comprehensive evaluation process led by an independent advisory firm,” according to Thursday’s announcement.

“In Ardent, we identified a partner that supports our mission, respects our culture and can accelerate our vision without compromising what makes Boone Health special,” Jerry Kennett, M.D., chair of Boone’s Board of Trustees, said in the announcement. "Following a thoughtful review, the Board is confident Ardent is the right long-term partner for Boone Health. This agreement protects what our community has built while giving Boone Health the resources it needs to grow."

Ardent Health’s current operations include 30 acute care hospitals and over 280 care sites, several of which are run as part of a joint venture structure with other health system partners. In 2025, the company increased its revenues by 6% to $6.3 billion and grew its adjusted EBITDA to $545 million.

The company recently logged a strong third quarter off the back of solid demand for emergent care and progress on its ongoing performance improvement initiative. In September, it also officially divested from a joint venture with the University of Kansas Health System, a decision that leadership said is part of an ongoing review of its portfolio to ensure long-term strategic fit among its hospitals and partnerships.

Serie A, 320 milioni da Avenue Capital ma alcuni club frenano: ecco perché l'accordo è a rischio

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Serie A, 320 milioni da Avenue Capital ma alcuni club frenano: ecco perché l'accordo è a rischio

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Si complica la trattativa tra la Lega Serie A e Avenue Capital per la cessione di una quota del business dei diritti televisivi internazionali. Secondo quanto riportato da Reuters, alcuni club del massimo campionato italiano avrebbero espresso perplessità sull'operazione, rallentando il percorso verso un possibile accordo.

Il fondo statunitense, con sede a New York, ha messo sul tavolo 320 milioni di euro per acquisire circa il 10% della nuova società destinata a gestire i diritti internazionali della Serie A, comprese le attività legate alle trasmissioni televisive, alle sponsorizzazioni e al settore delle scommesse. Una proposta che attribuisce alla società una valutazione complessiva di 3,2 miliardi di euro.

Non tutti i club, però, sono convinti di concedere ad Avenue Capital l'esclusiva nelle trattative. Secondo tre fonti interpellate da Reuters, alcune società ritengono che la Lega debba mantenere la possibilità di esaminare eventuali offerte alternative mentre approfondisce la proposta del fondo statunitense.

Al momento Avenue Capital rimane l'unico soggetto attivamente impegnato nel negoziato, pur senza aver ottenuto formalmente l'esclusiva. Le fonti non hanno specificato quali siano i club contrari né il loro numero, ma le resistenze emerse sarebbero sufficienti a rallentare l'operazione.

Per arrivare all'approvazione definitiva sarà necessario ottenere il voto favorevole di almeno 14 delle 20 società di Serie A.

Non sarebbe la prima volta che il calcio italiano tenta di aprire agli investitori esterni. Nel 2021 era stata valutata la cessione del 10% del business dei diritti televisivi nazionali a un consorzio guidato da CVC, ma il progetto era naufragato per la mancanza del consenso necessario tra le società.

Anche questa volta, dunque, la posizione dei club sarà determinante. L'offerta di Avenue Capital rimane sul tavolo, ma l'intesa è tutt'altro che raggiunta.

Frattesi commuove i tifosi dell'Inter: "San Siro sempre casa. Quella porta ha visto..."

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Frattesi commuove i tifosi dell'Inter: "San Siro sempre casa. Quella porta ha visto..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Ma i ricordi non passano mai. E Davide Frattesi lo sa benissimo. Perché ad un certo punto all'Inter non è riuscito più a trovare lo spazio che voleva ed è dovuto partire, tante voci, poi è arrivato la Lazio, è tornato alle sue origini. A casa. Ma casa per lui è stata anche l'Inter, San Siro, dove ieri ha giocato e segnato con la maglia dell'Italia e la sua rete è arrivata da un'azione architettata da Barella e Dimarco, suoi ex compagni.

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

E chi ha urlato con lui di gioia quella volta, quel gol se lo ricorda benissimo e lo hanno riempito di messaggi. "Ci mancava solo il cancello giallo, Davide, e poi ci mettevamo a piangere", "Se demoliscono San Siro il cancello giallo devono dartelo"; "Sarai sempre uno di noi, Davide". Un amore finito, le tracce che ha lasciato che restano per sempre. Anche se non è andata come doveva, come poteva.
